Since $(1-\chi(\frac{y}{8R_{4}}))$ is a smooth function and since $\nabla^{i}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{8R_{4}}))$ has compact support for each $i\geq 1$ , using the Leibniz rule and Proposition 3.8 , we obtain (4.21c) $\|e^{{\mathcal{L}}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}g\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+3}}\leq e^{-\lambda(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\|g\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+3}}\lesssim_{m}e^{-\lambda(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,{\mathsf{u}}}(s_{\mathsf{in}})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+3}}\lesssim_{m}e^{-\lambda(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}M.$ Combining the bounds obtained in ( 4.21a )–( 4.21c ) with the definition of the operator ${\mathcal{T}}_{2}$ via ( 4.4a ), and with the ordering ( 4.9 ) of the parameters, we complete the proof of the lemma. MM $\square$ 4.4.2. Proof of Proposition 4.3 Before proving Proposition 4.3 , we record the following product estimate for the nonlinear terms appearing on the right side of ( 4.2a ). Lemma 4.7 . Let ${\mathcal{N}}_{U},{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma},{\mathcal{N}}_{A}$ be the nonlinear terms defined in ( 2.7 ).
For any $k\geq m_{0}$ , we have $\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{2k}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\Big(\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\Delta^{k}\tilde{\Sigma}+\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\mathbf{A}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}}^{2},$ $\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{2k}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\Big(\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\tilde{\Sigma}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{k}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}}^{2},$ $\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{2k}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\Big(\Delta^{k}{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{A})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}}^{2}.$ Note that since $\varphi_{g}\lesssim\varphi_{A}$ , we may a-posteriori bound the norm $\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}}$ appearing on the right side of the above estimates with $\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}_{A}}$ . Proof. We estimate a typical term, for instance $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}}$ , appearing in ${\mathcal{N}}_{U}$ . Using the Leibniz rule, recalling the definitions of the weights $\varphi_{2k}$ (see ( 3.2 ) with ( 3.1a )) and $\varphi_{g}$ (see ( 3.15 )), and using Lemma C.4 , we have $\left\|\varphi_{2k}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\Bigl(\Delta^{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{k}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\Bigr)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{k}\sum_{1\leq i\leq 2k}\left\|\varphi_{2k}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}|\nabla^{i}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\cdot|\nabla^{2k+1-i}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{k}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{k}}^{2}.$ All other terms appearing in the nonlinear terms ${\mathcal{N}}_{U},{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}$ and ${\mathcal{N}}_{A}$ (see ( 2.7 )) are estimated similarly. MM $\square$ Next, we prove Proposition 4.3 . Recall the different notations from the beginning of Section 4 , in particular ( 4.1 ), and ( 4.5 ).
Per the assumption of Proposition 4.3 , let $\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{Y_{2}}<\delta_{Y}$ for some $\delta_{Y}$ small to be determined. Define $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}$ using ( 4.15 ), and then define $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}$ as the solution of ( 4.16 ). Denote (4.22a) $\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}$ $\displaystyle={\mathcal{T}}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1}),\quad\tilde{\mathbf{V}}=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2},\quad\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\quad\tilde{\Sigma}=\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{2},$ (4.22b) $\displaystyle\widehat{E}$ $\displaystyle=\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{Y_{2}}<\delta_{Y},$ (4.22c) $\displaystyle E_{m+1}(s)$ $\displaystyle\triangleq\bigl(\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}(s),\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}(s))\|_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2},$ (4.22d) $\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}(s)$ $\displaystyle=E_{m+1}(s)+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}.$ Then, by the above definitions and ( 4.7 ) we have (4.23) $\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}}+\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}_{A}^{m+1}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\lesssim_{m}\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}}+\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}_{A}^{m+1}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}.$ Note that since $\varphi_{g}\lesssim\varphi_{A}$ , the bound ( 4.23 ) also gives $\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}$ , an estimate we use occasionally. The term $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}$ is already bounded in light of Lemma 4.6 . In order to estimate $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1},\mathbf{A})$ , we performing ${\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}$ -energy estimates on ( 4.16 ), to obtain (4.24a) $\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m+1}^{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{ds}\bigl(\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}}^{2}\bigr)=I_{{\mathcal{L}}}+I_{{\mathcal{N}}},$ (4.24b) $\displaystyle I_{{\mathcal{L}}}$ $\displaystyle\triangleq\langle({\mathcal{L}}-{\mathcal{K}}_{m})(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}),(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}+\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{A}(\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{A}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}},$ (4.24c) $\displaystyle I_{{\mathcal{N}}}$ $\displaystyle\triangleq\langle({\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})),(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}+\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\mathbf{A}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}_{A}}.$ For the linear contributions appearing in ( 4.24b ), using ( 4.8 ), we obtain the dissipative bound 28 28 28 Here we use that the solution $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}$ of ( 4.16 ) has sufficient regularity to justify the applicability of ( 4.8 ); see Footnote 27 . (4.25) $\displaystyle I_{{\mathcal{L}}}\leq$ $\displaystyle-\lambda_{1}\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}^{2}-\lambda_{1}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}}^{2}=-\lambda_{1}E_{m+1}^{2}.$ Thus, it is left to estimate the contribution to ( 4.24a ) from the nonlinear terms in ( 4.24c ). Estimate of nonlinear terms. We treat the nonlinear terms in ( 4.24c ) perturbatively. Our goal is to prove (4.26) $|I_{{\mathcal{N}}}|\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}.$ We first estimate the contribution to ( 4.24c ) from the nonlinear term ${\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})$ , focusing on the terms containing most derivatives, with respect to the ${\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}$ -inner product. Using Lemma 4.7 , the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the bound ( 4.23 ) to absorb the lower-order terms, we get $\displaystyle I_{{\mathcal{N}},U}$ $\displaystyle\triangleq\int\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\Delta^{m+1}{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}$ $\displaystyle=-\int\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\Big(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}+\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}\Big)\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\mathcal{O}_{m}({\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}).$ Since $\varphi_{k}=\varphi_{1}^{k},\varphi_{1}\asymp\langle y\rangle\gtrsim 1$ , using Lemma C.4 (with $i=0$ , $j=2m+3$ , $m\to m+1$ , $n=m+2$ ), and the bound ( 4.23 ), for $G\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ and $F\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\tilde{\Sigma}_{2}\}$ we get $\|\varphi_{2m+2}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}G\nabla\Delta^{m+1}F\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\|\langle y\rangle^{2m+2}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}G\nabla\Delta^{m+1}F\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\|G\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}}\|F\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}^{2}.$ Thus, combining the two displayed estimates above, we can reduce bounding $I_{{\mathcal{N}},U}$ to bounding the main terms, which involve $\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}$ ; that is, we have $I_{{\mathcal{N}},U}=-\int\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\Big(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}\Big)\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\mathcal{O}_{m}({\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}).$ In order to estimate the contribution of the transport term $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}$ , using Lemma 3.1 , the definition of $\varphi_{2m+2}$ in ( 3.2 ), the definition of $\varphi_{g}$ in ( 3.15 ), estimate ( 4.23 ), and the embedding ( C.5 ), we obtain (4.27a) $\displaystyle|\nabla(\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g})|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\langle y\rangle^{-1},$ (4.27b) $\displaystyle|\nabla(\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}G)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\bigl(\langle y\rangle^{-1}|G|+|\nabla G|\bigr)\lesssim_{m}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}{\mathcal{E}},$ for any $G\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ .
Since $(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla)|\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}|^{2}$ , using integration by parts and ( 4.27 ) we obtain $\Big|\int\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\Big|\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}\int\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}|\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}|^{2}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}E_{m+1}^{2}.$ For the lower order $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}$ -terms (which already contain the $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}^{0}}$ -contribution) present in the inner product $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}$ (see ( 4.7 )), using Lemma C.4 and estimate ( 4.23 ), we get $\|{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\lesssim_{m}\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}}^{2}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}^{2}.$ Upon recalling the definition ( 4.7 ) of the inner product ${\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}$ , and the definitions of the inner products ${\mathcal{X}}^{m+1}$ and ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}$ in ( 3.14 ), we may combine the above estimates to obtain $\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}=\varepsilon_{m+1}\mu_{m+1}I_{{\mathcal{N}},U}+\mathcal{O}_{m}(\|{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}})$ (4.28) $\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{m+1}\mu_{m+1}\int\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m}^{2}\bigl(\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+(\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}\bigr)\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\mathcal{O}_{m}({\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}).$ In a similar fashion, for the contribution to ( 4.24c ) from the nonlinear term ${\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})$ , by using Lemma 4.7 and the same arguments/estimates as above, we may obtain (4.29) $\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}$ $\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{m+1}\mu_{m+1}\!\int\!\varphi_{g}\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\bigl(\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\bigr)\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\!+\!\mathcal{O}_{m}({\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}).$ It thus remains to estimate the contribution to ( 4.24c ) from the nonlinear term ${\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})$ . Note that the inner products on ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}_{A}$ and ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}$ , and hence ${\mathcal{W}}^{m}_{A}$ and ${\mathcal{W}}^{m}$ , differ only at the weighted- $L^{2}$ level, where the weight $\varphi_{g}$ needs to be replaced by $\varphi_{A}$ ; see definitions ( 3.14 ) and ( 4.7 ). At this weighted- $L^{2}$ level, using ( 4.23 ) and the embedding ( C.5 ), we first obtain $|\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|+|y|^{-1}|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\lesssim_{m}\|\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}.$ Then, using integration by parts and the bound $|\nabla\varphi_{A}|\lesssim|y|^{-1}\varphi_{A}$ (see ( 3.28 )), we deduce $\Bigl|\int{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})\cdot\mathbf{A}\varphi_{A}\Bigr|\leq\int\Big(\Big|\frac{\nabla\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\varphi_{A})}{2\varphi_{A}}\Big|+\|\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)|\mathbf{A}|^{2}\varphi_{A}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{0}_{A}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}E_{m+1}^{2}.$ For the terms in $\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\mathbf{A}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}}$ which contain derivatives, the bounds and arguments are nearly identical to those of employed earlier to handle $\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}$ . Thus, using the same arguments as those leading up to ( 4.28 ) and ( 4.29 ), and by appealing to the estimate displayed above, we may show (4.30) $\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\mathbf{A}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1}}=-\varepsilon_{m+1}\mu_{m+1}\!\int\!\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\bigl((\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\bigr)\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}+\mathcal{O}_{m}({\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}).$ To conclude, we estimate the term $I_{{\mathcal{N}}}$ defined in ( 4.24c ) by combining the bounds ( 4.28 ), ( 4.29 ), and ( 4.30 ). Using the identities (4.31a) $\displaystyle(\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}+(\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla)\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}$ $\displaystyle=(\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla)(\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}),$ (4.31b) $\displaystyle\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}$ $\displaystyle=\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\;\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}),$ integration by parts, and the estimates ( 4.27 ), we establish $\displaystyle|I_{{\mathcal{N}}}|=\bigl|\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{U}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}+\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}}+\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),\mathbf{A}\rangle_{{\mathcal{W}}^{m+1}_{A}}\bigr|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\int|\operatorname{div}(\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\mathbf{A})||\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}|+\alpha|\nabla(\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\tilde{\Sigma})|\cdot|\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}|+{\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}$ (4.32) $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}\int\varphi_{2m+2}^{2}\varphi_{g}\bigl(|\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\cdot\Delta^{m+1}\mathbf{A}|+|\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\Delta^{m+1}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}|\bigr)+{\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}\lesssim_{m}{\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}.$ This concludes the proof of ( 4.26 ). Conclusion of the proof. Combining ( 4.24a ), ( 4.25 ), and ( 4.26 ), we obtain the energy estimate $\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m+1}^{2}\leq-\lambda_{1}E_{m+1}^{2}+C_{m}{\mathcal{E}}^{2}E_{m+1}.$ Applying Lemma 4.6 , recalling that $\widehat{E}=\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{Y_{2}}$ , and using the definition of ${\mathcal{E}}$ in ( 4.22 ), we obtain (4.33) ${\mathcal{E}}\leq E_{m+1}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}=E_{m+1}+\|{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1})(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\leq E_{m+1}+C_{m}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\hat{E}.$ From the above two estimates we thus deduce (4.34) $\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m+1}\leq-\lambda_{1}E_{m+1}+\bar{C}_{m}E_{m+1}^{2}+\bar{C}_{m}e^{-2\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\hat{E}^{2},$ for some constant $\bar{C}_{m}>0$ . We consider the bootstrap assumption (4.35) $E_{m+1}(s)<2\delta e^{-\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})},\qquad\forall s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}.$ Using the assumption ( 4.10 ) in Theorem 4.1 , we have $E_{m+1}(s_{\mathsf{in}})<\delta$ , so that ( 4.35 ) holds at $s=s_{\mathsf{in}}$ . We also recall from ( 4.22 ) and ( 4.19 ) that by assumption $\hat{E}<\delta_{Y}=\delta^{2/3}$ . Thus, since $2\eta_{s}-\lambda_{1}>0$ (see ( 4.9 )), by integrating ( 4.34 ) in time, and using the bootstrap ( 4.35 ), we deduce that for all $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ : $\displaystyle E_{m+1}(s)$ $\displaystyle\leq\delta+\bar{C}_{m}\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{\infty}\Bigl(4\delta^{2}e^{-\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}+\delta_{Y}^{2}e^{-(2\eta_{s}-\lambda_{1})(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\Bigr)ds$ $\displaystyle=\delta+\bar{C}_{m}\bigl(4\lambda_{1}^{-1}\delta^{2}+(2\eta_{s}-\lambda_{1})^{-1}\delta^{4/3})=\bigl(1+\tfrac{40}{9}\lambda^{-1}\bar{C}_{m}\delta+\tfrac{10}{3}\lambda^{-1}\bar{C}_{m}\delta^{1/3}\bigr)\delta.$ Choosing $\delta_{0}\ll_{m}1$ small enough to ensure $\tfrac{40}{9}\delta_{0}+\tfrac{10}{3}\delta_{0}^{1/3}\leq\lambda\bar{C}_{m}^{-1}$ , we deduce from the above estimate that for any $\delta<\delta_{0}$ , the bootstrap assumption ( 4.35 ) is closed. Recalling the definition of the $Y_{1}$ norm in ( 4.13a ), it is clear that ( 4.35 ) is equivalent to the first estimate in Proposition 4.3 . From ( 4.7 ) and ( 4.13 ) it follows that $\|\cdot\|_{Y_{2}}\leq\|\cdot\|_{Y_{1}}$ , and hence ( 4.35 ) gives (4.36) $\|{\mathcal{T}}_{U,\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{Y_{2}}\leq\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1})\|_{Y_{1}}\leq{\sup}_{s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}}e^{\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}E_{m+1}(s)<2\delta<(2\delta_{0}^{1/3})\delta_{Y}.$ The second bound in Proposition 4.3 thus follows as soon as $2\delta_{0}^{1/3}\leq 1$ . The third estimate claimed in Proposition 4.3 was previously established in Lemma 4.6 .
This concludes the proof of Proposition 4.3 . 4.4.3. Proof of Proposition 4.4 As in the assumption of the proposition, let $\{\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell}\}_{\ell\in\{a,b\}}\in Y_{2}$ be such that $\hat{E}_{\ell}=\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell}\|_{Y_{2}}<\delta_{Y}$ . According to ( 4.15 ), ( 4.16 ), and ( 4.17 ), denote the associated solutions $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\ell}={\mathcal{T}}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell})=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2,\ell},\tilde{\Sigma}_{2,\ell},0),\quad\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,\ell}={\mathcal{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell})=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\ell},\mathbf{A}_{\ell}),\quad\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{\ell}=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,\ell}+\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\ell},$ for $\ell\in\{a,b\}$ .
Here and throughout this proof, we use the subscript $\ell\in\{a,b\}$ to denote two different solutions, and we adopt the notation introduced ( 4.22 ); e.g. $E_{m+1,\ell}$ and ${\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}$ , for the “energies” of these two solutions. Additionally, we denote the difference of two solutions by a $\Delta$ -sub-index: (4.37a) $\displaystyle(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})=(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,a},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,a})-(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,b},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,b})$ (4.37b) $\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,\Delta}=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,a}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,b},\quad\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\Delta}=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,a}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,b},\quad{\mathcal{N}}_{V,\Delta}={\mathcal{N}}_{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{a})-{\mathcal{N}}_{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{b}),$ (4.37c) $\displaystyle E_{m,\Delta}(s)+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\Delta}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}.$ With this notation, proving Proposition 4.4 amounts to showing that (4.38) Similarly to ( 4.24 ), appealing to ( 3.30 ) for ${\mathcal{L}}-{\mathcal{K}}_{m}$ and to ( 3.13b ) for ${\mathcal{L}}_{A}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m,\Delta}^{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{ds}\Big(\|(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}_{A}}^{2}\Big)$ $\displaystyle=\langle({\mathcal{L}}-{\mathcal{K}}_{m})(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta}),(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}+\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{A}(\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}),\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}_{A}^{m}}+I_{{\mathcal{N}},\Delta}$ ${{\mathcal{N}}}_{A,\Delta}$ to ( 4.40b ),
by using ( 4.42b )–( 4.42c ), ( 4.43a ), ( 4.43b ), the Leibniz rule, Lemma 4.7 , and Lemma C.4 , similarly to the proofs of ( 4.29 )–( 4.30 ), we obtain $\displaystyle I_{\Sigma,\Delta}=\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{S,\Delta},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta}\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}$ $\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{m}\int\varphi_{2m}^{2}\varphi_{g}\bigl(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{b}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta}+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}_{b}\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{m}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta})\bigr)\cdot\Delta^{m}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta}$ (4.44b) $\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}_{m}\bigl(({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}){\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}E_{m,\Delta}\bigr),$ $\displaystyle I_{A,\Delta}=\langle{\mathcal{N}}_{A,\Delta},\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}\rangle_{{\mathcal{X}}_{A}^{m}}$ $\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{m}\int\varphi_{2m}^{2}\varphi_{g}\bigl(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{b}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m}\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}+\mathbf{A}_{b}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta}\bigr)\cdot\Delta^{m}\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}$ (4.44c) $\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}_{m}\bigl(({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}){\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}E_{m,\Delta}\bigr).$ Finally, we sum the three identities in ( 4.44 ), appeal to the vector calculus identities in ( 4.31 ), integrate by parts, use ( 4.27 ) with $2m+2$ replaced by $2m$ and with $G\in\{(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\ell},\tilde{\Sigma}_{\ell},\mathbf{A}_{\ell})\colon\ell\in\{a,b\}\}$ , and appeal to the bound ( 4.43a ), to conclude $\displaystyle I_{{\mathcal{N}},\Delta}$ $\displaystyle=I_{U,\Delta}+I_{\Sigma,\Delta}+I_{A,\Delta}$ $\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{m}\int\varphi_{2m}^{2}\varphi_{g}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{b}\cdot\nabla)\bigl|\Delta^{m}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1,\Delta}\bigr|^{2}$ $\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}_{b}\operatorname{div}\bigl(\Delta^{m}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta}\Delta^{m}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta}\bigr)+\mathbf{A}_{b}\cdot\nabla\bigl(\Delta^{m}\mathbf{A}_{\Delta}\cdot\Delta^{m}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta}\bigr)\Bigr)$ $\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}_{m}\bigl(({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}){\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}E_{m,\Delta}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{m}\bigl(({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b})E_{m,\Delta}^{2}\bigr)+\mathcal{O}_{m}\bigl(({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}){\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}E_{m,\Delta}\bigr).$ This concludes the proof of ( 4.41 ). Conclusion of the proof. Combining the estimates ( 4.40 )–( 4.41 ), we deduce (4.45) $\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m,\Delta}+\lambda E_{m,\Delta}\lesssim_{m}({\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}){\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta},$ where we recall that ${\mathcal{E}}_{a},{\mathcal{E}}_{b},{\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}$ are defined in ( 4.37 ).
In order to bound the right side of ( 4.45 ), we note that since ${\mathcal{T}}_{2}$ is a linear map, $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\Delta}={\mathcal{T}}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})$ , and so using Lemma 4.6 , we obtain ${\mathcal{E}}_{\Delta}(s)-E_{m,\Delta}(s)=\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2,\Delta}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\lesssim_{m}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{Y_{2}}.$ On the other hand, for the term ${\mathcal{E}}_{a}+{\mathcal{E}}_{b}$ , using ( 4.33 ) and ( 4.35 ) (applicable since $\{(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell})\}_{\ell\in\{a,b\}}$ satisfy the assumptions of Proposition 4.3 ), we obtain ${\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}(s)=E_{m+1,\ell}+\|{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\ell},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\ell})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}}\leq 2\delta e^{-\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}+C_{m}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\delta_{Y},$ for $\ell\in\{a,b\}$ .
From the two estimates above and ( 4.45 ), we obtain $\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m,\Delta}+\lambda E_{m,\Delta}\leq\tilde{C}_{m}(\delta e^{-\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}+e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\delta^{2/3})\bigl(E_{m,\Delta}+e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{Y_{2}}\bigr),$ for some constant $\tilde{C}_{m}\geq 1$ . Since $\eta_{s}<\lambda_{1}<\lambda$ (see ( 4.9 )), we may choose $\delta_{0}\in(0,1]$ small enough to ensure $\tilde{C}_{m}(\delta_{0}+\delta_{0}^{2/3})\leq\lambda-\lambda_{1}$ , and thus, for all $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ and any $\delta\leq\delta_{0}$ we have $\tfrac{d}{ds}E_{m,\Delta}+\lambda_{1}E_{m,\Delta}\leq 2\tilde{C}_{m}\delta_{0}^{2/3}e^{-2\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{Y_{2}}.$ To conclude, recall that $E_{m,\Delta}(s_{\mathsf{in}})=0$ , and $2\eta_{s}>\lambda_{1}$ (see ( 4.9 )), so that by integrating the above estimate we arrive at $e^{\lambda_{1}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}E_{m,\Delta}(s)\leq 2\tilde{C}_{m}\delta_{0}^{2/3}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{Y_{2}}\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{\infty}e^{-(2\eta_{s}-\lambda_{1})(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}ds=\tfrac{20}{3}\lambda^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m}\delta_{0}^{2/3}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{1,\Delta},\widehat{\Sigma}_{1,\Delta})\|_{Y_{2}}.$ Upon further decreasing $\delta_{0}$ , to also ensure that $\frac{20}{3}\lambda^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m}\delta_{0}^{2/3}<\frac{1}{2}$ , the above estimate proves ( 4.38 ), thus concluding the proof of Proposition 4.4 . 4.5. Additional estimates of the solution from Theorem 1.1 In this section we establish sharp spatial decay estimates for the self-similar solution $(\mathbf{U},\Sigma,\mathbf{A})=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}+\bar{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{2}+\bar{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})$ constructed in Theorem 4.1 , with initial data as specified in ( 4.12 ); that is, for the self-similar solution described by Theorem 1.1 .
We claim that: (4.46a) $\displaystyle|\mathbf{U}(y,s)|+|\mathbf{A}(y,s)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{1-r}$ (4.46b) $\displaystyle|\nabla\mathbf{U}(y,s)|+|\nabla\Sigma(y,s)|+|\nabla\mathbf{A}(y,s)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-r},$ pointwise for $y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}$ , for all $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ , where we recall that $r>1$ (see ( 2.2 )). Note that ( 4.46a ) is missing a decay estimate for $\Sigma$ ; this is due to the fact that we have chosen the rescaled sound speed $\Sigma$ to equal a constant in the far-field at the initial time (see ( 4.12b )). The purpose of the decay estimates ( 4.46 ) is to prove that there are no singularities in the Euler solution $(\mathbf{u},\sigma)$ (in original $(x,t)$ variables) away from $(x,t)=(0,T)$ , as claimed in Remark 1.4 . Indeed, using the decay estimates in ( 4.46 ) and the self-similar relation ( 2.1 ), which gives $x=y(T-t)^{1/r}$ and $T-t=e^{-rs}$ , for any $t\in[0,T)$ and $x\neq 0$ (so that $|y|=|x|(T-t)^{-1/r}\to\infty$ as $t\scalebox{0.6}{$\nearrow$}T$ ) we obtain $\displaystyle|f(x,t)|$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{r}(T-t)^{\frac{1}{r}-1}|F(y,s)|$ (4.47a) $\displaystyle\lesssim(T-t)^{\frac{1}{r}-1}\langle y\rangle^{1-r}\leq|x|^{1-r},\qquad\forall(f,F)\in\bigl\{(\mathbf{u}^{R},\mathbf{U}),(\mathbf{u}^{\theta},\mathbf{A})\bigr\},$ $\displaystyle|(\nabla f)(x,t)|$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{r}(T-t)^{-1}|\nabla F(s,y)|$ (4.47b) $\displaystyle\lesssim(T-t)^{-1}\langle y\rangle^{-r}\leq|x|^{-r},\qquad\forall(f,F)\in\bigl\{(\mathbf{u}^{R},\mathbf{U}),(\sigma,\Sigma),(\mathbf{u}^{\theta},\mathbf{A})\bigr\}.$ As a consequence of ( 4.47 ), we have that $\mathbf{u},\nabla\mathbf{u},\nabla\sigma$ may only blow up at the origin $x=0$ , at time $t=T$ . In order to control the undifferentiated rescaled sound speed (missing from ( 4.47 )), we estimate $\sigma$ via the Lagrangian flow associated to $\mathbf{u}$ . By choosing $\delta$ to be sufficiently small in Theorem 4.1 , the estimates established in Theorem 4.1 imply that the total self-similar radial velocity $\mathbf{U}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}+\bar{\mathbf{U}}$ satisfies the assumption of Lemma A.1 . Let $a\mapsto X(a,t)$ be the flow map (recall, Footnote 16 ) associated with $\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{R}+\mathbf{u}^{\theta}$ , where $\mathbf{u}^{R}$ and $\mathbf{u}^{\theta}$ are given in terms of $\mathbf{U}$ and $\mathbf{A}$ via ( 2.1b )–( 2.1c ). Fix $x\neq 0$ . For any $0\leq t<T$ , denote $x_{0}=X^{-1}(x,t)$ , i.e. $X(x_{0},t)=x$ .
From Lemma A.1 , for $0\leq t^{\prime}<t<T$ , we have (4.48) $|x|=|X(x_{0},t)|\lesssim|x_{0}|,\quad R_{l}(x)\triangleq|x|\min(1,|x|^{c_{1}})\lesssim|x_{0}|\min(1,|x_{0}|^{c_{1}})\lesssim|X(x_{0},t^{\prime})|\lesssim|x_{0}|,$ for some positive constant $c_{1}$ . In particular, the above bounds imply that $x\neq 0\Leftrightarrow x_{0}\neq 0$ .
Next, we use ( 1.3b ) and the definition $x=X(x_{0},t)$ to write $\sigma(x,t)=\sigma_{0}(x_{0})\exp\Bigl(-\alpha\int_{0}^{t}(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u})(X(x_{0},t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\Bigr).$ Since for axisymmetric flows we have $(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u})(x^{\prime},t)=(\tfrac{1}{R}+\partial_{R})u^{R}(|x^{\prime}|,t)$ , by ( 4.47 ) and ( 4.48 ) it follows that $|(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u})(X(x_{0},t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})|\lesssim|X(x_{0},t^{\prime})|^{-r}\leq R_{l}(x)^{-r}$ , for all $t^{\prime}\in[0,t]$ . Therefore, we deduce (4.49) $\|\sigma_{0}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1}\exp\bigl(-CR_{l}(x)^{-r}\bigr)\leq\sigma(x,t)\leq\|\sigma_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\exp\bigl(CR_{l}(x)^{-r}\bigr)$ for all $x\neq 0$ and $t\in[0,T)$ , for some constant $C>0$ which is allowed to depend on $T$ but not on $x$ .
In particular, we obtain that $\sigma(x,t)$ does not become vacuous and does not blow up away from $(x,t)=(0,T)$ . The goal of the remaining part of this section is to prove ( 4.46 ). 4.5.1. Sharp decay estimates of the solution Recall cf. ( 2.4a ) that the steady state $(\bar{\mathbf{U}},\bar{\Sigma},0)$ satisfies the bounds ( 4.46 ). We thus only need to perform weighted $W^{i,\infty}$ estimates, for $i\in\{0,1\}$ , on the perturbation $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}=(\mathbf{U}-\bar{\mathbf{U}},\Sigma-\bar{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A})=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}$ (see ( 4.1 )). Adding the equations for $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}$ and $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}$ in ( 4.2 ), we obtain $\partial_{s}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}={\mathcal{L}}_{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})+{\mathcal{N}}_{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}),$ where ${\mathcal{L}}_{V}=({\mathcal{L}}_{U},{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma},{\mathcal{L}}_{A})$ and ${\mathcal{N}}_{V}=({\mathcal{N}}_{U},{\mathcal{N}}_{A},{\mathcal{N}}_{\Sigma})$ are defined in ( 2.6 ). In order to establish ( 4.46 ), we consider the weighted quantity (4.50a) $F\triangleq(\phi_{0}^{2}(|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|^{2}+|\mathbf{A}|^{2})+\phi_{1}^{2}|\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{V}}|^{2})^{1/2},\qquad\mbox{where}\qquad\phi_{0}=\langle y\rangle^{r-1},\quad\phi_{1}=\langle y\rangle^{r},$ and note that ( 4.46 ) is a consequence of the uniform boundedness of the associated “energy” (4.50b) $E_{\infty}(s)\triangleq\|F(\cdot,s)\|_{L^{\infty}},$ for $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ .
Note that since $\tilde{\Sigma}$ does not decay, we do not include the term $\phi_{0}|\tilde{\Sigma}|$ in the definition of $F$ . In order to estimate $E_{\infty}(s)$ , for $f\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ we first focus on the linear terms $(r-1)f$ and $y\cdot\nabla f$ present in ( 2.6d )–( 2.6f ); accordingly, we re-write ( 2.6a )–( 2.6c ) as (4.51a) $\displaystyle\partial_{s}f+y\cdot\nabla f$ $\displaystyle=(1-r)f+{\mathcal{L}}_{f,R}+{\mathcal{N}}_{f},$ (4.51b) $\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{U,R}$ $\displaystyle=-\bar{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\bar{\mathbf{U}}-\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\bar{\Sigma}-\alpha\bar{\Sigma}\nabla\tilde{\Sigma},$ (4.51c) $\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{A,R}$ $\displaystyle=-\bar{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\cdot\nabla\bar{\mathbf{U}},$ (4.51d) $\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma,R}$ $\displaystyle=-\bar{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Sigma}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\nabla\bar{\Sigma}-\alpha\tilde{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\bar{\mathbf{U}})-\alpha\bar{\Sigma}\operatorname{div}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})$ for terms ${\mathcal{L}}_{f,R},{\mathcal{N}}_{f}$ , which are quadratic in either the perturbation or the profile, and which decay faster than the linear terms; we will treat these perturbatively and think of them as lower order. For $(i,f)\in\{(0,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(0,\mathbf{A}),(1,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(1,\tilde{\Sigma}),(1,\mathbf{A})\}$ , a direct computation yields (4.52) $\partial_{s}(\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}f)+y\cdot\nabla(\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}f)=\Bigl((1-r)-i+\frac{y\cdot\nabla\phi_{i}}{\phi_{i}}\Bigr)(\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}f)+\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}({\mathcal{L}}_{f,R}f+{\mathcal{N}}_{f}).$ Since $r>1$ we may directly verify that (4.53) $(1-r)-i+\frac{y\cdot\nabla\phi_{i}}{\phi_{i}}=(1-r)-i+(r-1+i)\frac{|y|^{2}}{\langle y\rangle^{2}}=-(r-1+i)\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{2}}\leq 0$ for all $y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}$ .
For the contribution of the lower order terms present on the right side of ( 4.52 ), in Sections 4.5.2 and 4.5.3 below, we will establish the pointwise estimates (4.54) $|\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}{\mathcal{L}}_{f,R}|\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}},\quad|\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}{\mathcal{N}}_{f}|\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}^{2},$ for all $(i,f)\in\{(0,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(0,\mathbf{A}),(1,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(1,\tilde{\Sigma}),(1,\mathbf{A})\}$ . Multiplying equation ( 4.52 ) with $\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}f$ , summing over $(i,f)\in\{(0,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(0,\mathbf{A}),(1,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(1,\tilde{\Sigma}),(1,\mathbf{A})\}$ , recalling the definition of $F$ in ( 4.50 ), and combining the resulting identity with the bounds ( 4.53 ) and ( 4.54 ), we deduce $\partial_{s}F^{2}+y\cdot\nabla F^{2}\leq C\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}(1+\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}})F.$ Upon dividing both sides by $F$ , composing with the Lagrangian flow of the operator $\partial_{s}+y\cdot\nabla$ , which is given explicitly as $Y(y_{0},s)=y_{0}e^{s-s_{\mathsf{in}}}$ , and using the nonlinear estimate $\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}(s)\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\lesssim\delta^{2/3}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}$ established 29 29 29 The norms ${\mathcal{W}}^{m+1},{\mathcal{W}}_{A}^{m+1},{\mathcal{X}}^{m+2}$ are stronger than the ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}$ norm; see ( 3.14 ), the bound $\varphi_{g}\lesssim\varphi_{A}$ , Lemma 3.3 , and ( 4.7 ). earlier in Theorem 4.1 , we obtain $\tfrac{d}{ds}F(Y(y_{0},s),s)\leq C\delta^{2/3}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}(1+C\delta^{2/3}e^{-\eta_{s}(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})})$ for some $C>0$ independent of $\delta$ and of $(y_{0},s)$ . Using that by definition $F(y_{0},s_{\mathsf{in}})\leq E_{\infty}(s_{\mathsf{in}})$ for all $y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$ , integrating the above ODE with respect to $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ , then taking the supremum over $y_{0}$ (here we use that the map $y_{0}\mapsto Y(y_{0},s)$ is a bijection of $\mathbb{R}^{2}$ for any $s$ ) we arrive at $\displaystyle E_{\infty}(s)$ $\displaystyle\leq E_{\infty}(s_{\mathsf{in}})+C\delta^{2/3}\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s}e^{-\eta_{s}(s^{\prime}-s_{\mathsf{in}})}ds^{\prime}+C^{2}\delta^{4/3}\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s}e^{-2\eta_{s}(s^{\prime}-s_{\mathsf{in}})}ds^{\prime}$ $\displaystyle\leq E_{\infty}(s_{\mathsf{in}})+C\delta^{2/3}\eta_{s}^{-1}+C\delta^{4/3}(2\eta_{s})^{-1},$ for all $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ .
Combining the above estimate with the boundedness of $E_{\infty}(s_{\mathsf{in}})$ (which follows from the definition of the initial data in ( 4.12 ), from the decay of the self-similar profiles $(\bar{U},\bar{\Sigma})$ in ( 2.4a ), and from the fact that $\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}(s_{\mathsf{in}})$ has compact support ( 4.4d )), we deduce that the function $F$ defined in ( 4.50a ) is uniformly bounded in space and time, thereby proving ( 4.46 ). It only remains to establish ( 4.54 ), which we achieve in the next two subsections. 4.5.2. Estimates of the linear terms In this section, we establish the first estimate in ( 4.54 ), which concerns the linear terms defined in ( 4.51b )–( 4.51d ). From Lemma C.4 , we have (4.55) $|\nabla^{i}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}|\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-i+\kappa_{1}/2},\qquad\kappa_{1}=\tfrac{1}{4},\quad 0\leq i\leq 2m-2.$ For the sake of brevity, we estimate only two “typical” terms appearing in the definition of $\phi_{i}\nabla^{i}{\mathcal{L}}_{f,R}$ for $(i,f)\in\{(0,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(0,\mathbf{A}),(1,\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),(1,\tilde{\Sigma}),(1,\mathbf{A})\}$ ; see ( 4.51b )–( 4.51d ). More precisely, we consider $\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\bar{\Sigma}$ and $\bar{\Sigma}\nabla\tilde{\Sigma}$ , which arise when $f=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}$ . Recall the decay estimates of the profile in ( 2.4a ) and the fact that $\kappa_{1}\in(0,1)$ . Applying ( 4.55 ), for $i\in\{0,1\}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}|\nabla^{i}(\tilde{\Sigma}\nabla\bar{\Sigma})|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}(|\nabla^{i}\tilde{\Sigma}|\;|\nabla\bar{\Sigma}|+|\tilde{\Sigma}|\;|\nabla^{1+i}\bar{\Sigma}|)$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-i+\kappa_{1}/2-r}\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}},$ $\displaystyle\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}|\nabla^{i}(\bar{\Sigma}\nabla\tilde{\Sigma})|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}(|\nabla^{i}\bar{\Sigma}|\;|\nabla\tilde{\Sigma}|+|\bar{\Sigma}|\;|\nabla^{1+i}\tilde{\Sigma}|)$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{i+r-1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-i+\kappa_{1}/2-r}\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}.$ All the other terms present in ( 4.51b )–( 4.51d ) are estimated similarly; we omit these details and conclude the proof of the first estimate in ( 4.54 ). 4.5.3. Estimates of the nonlinear terms In this section, we establish the second estimate in ( 4.54 ), which concerns the nonlinear terms defined in ( 2.7 ). We note that each nonlinear terms ${\mathcal{N}}_{f}$ in ( 2.7 ) can be written as a sum of terms of the kind $F\nabla G$ for $F,G\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ . By
using estimate ( 4.55 ), we obtain $\phi_{0}|F\nabla G|\lesssim\phi_{0}\;\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{\kappa_{1}/2}\;\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-1+\kappa_{1}/2}=\langle y\rangle^{r-2+\kappa_{1}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}^{2}.$ At this stage, we recall from ( 3.15 ) that $\kappa_{1}=1/4$ , and from ( 2.2 ) that $r<r_{\mathsf{eye}}(\alpha)$ . Moreover, since ( 2.2 ) gives an explicit expression for $r_{\mathsf{eye}}(\alpha)$ , we may directly verify that $r_{\mathsf{eye}}(\alpha)<\sqrt{2}$ for all $\alpha>0$ . Therefore, (4.56) $r-2+\kappa_{1}<\sqrt{2}-2+\tfrac{1}{4}<0.$ The above two bounds proves the second estimate in ( 4.54 ) for $i=0$ . It thus remains to consider the second bound in ( 4.54 ) for $i=1$ and $f\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ . We proceed as above, using ( 4.50 ) and ( 4.55 ) we deduce $\displaystyle\phi_{1}|\nabla(F\nabla G)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{1}|\nabla F|\,|\nabla G|+\phi_{1}|F|\,|\nabla^{2}G|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\phi_{1}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-1+\kappa_{1}/2}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-1+\kappa_{1}/2}+\phi_{1}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{\kappa_{1}/2}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-2+\kappa_{1}/2}$ $\displaystyle=\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}^{2}\langle y\rangle^{r-2+\kappa_{1}}.$ Appealing to ( 4.56 ), the above estimate yields $\phi_{1}|\nabla(F\nabla G)|\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}^{2}$ for $F,G\in\{\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{\Sigma},\mathbf{A}\}$ . This concludes the proof of ( 4.54 ). Appendix A Trajectory estimates and the proof of Lemma 2.4 In this section, we first establish a trajectory estimate and then prove Lemma 2.4 using the properties of the profile in Lemma 2.2 . Lemma A.1 . Assume that the physical space velocity $\mathbf{u}$ is self-similar according to the transformation ( 2.1 ). In particular let $\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{U}(y,s)=U(|y|,s)\mathbf{e}_{R}$ denote the self-similar radial velocity, and recall that $x=ye^{-s}$ and $T-t=e^{-rs}$ .
Suppose that $\mathbf{U}\in C^{1}_{y,s}$ and that $\mathbf{U}$ satisfies the quantitative bound (A.1) $|\mathbf{U}(s,y)-\bar{\mathbf{U}}(y)|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\min(\kappa,\tfrac{r-1}{2\alpha})\min(|y|,|y|^{1-\varepsilon}),$ for all $y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$ , $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ , and some $\varepsilon>0$ , where $\bar{\mathbf{U}}=\bar{U}\mathbf{e}_{R}$ and the parameters $\kappa,r,\alpha>0$ satisfy ( 2.4 ). Then, there exists a sufficiently small $c_{1}>0$ , with $c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,\kappa,r,\alpha,\bar{U},T)$ , such that the Lagrangian flow map $a\mapsto X(a,t)$ associated with $\mathbf{u}(x,t)$ in physical space variables, satisfies the uniform estimates (A.2) $C^{-1}|a|\min(1,|a|^{c_{1}})\leq|X(a,t)|\leq C|a|,$ for all $a\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$ and all $t\in[0,T)$ , for some constant $C=C(\varepsilon,\kappa,r,\alpha,\bar{U},T)\geq 1$ . Proof. Fix $a\neq 0$ . According to ( 2.1a ), define the radial component of the corresponding self-similar trajectory to be $R(s)\triangleq\frac{|X(a,t)|}{(T-t)^{1/r}}=|X(a,t)|e^{s}.$ Since $\mathbf{U}(y,s)=U(|y|,s)\mathbf{e}_{R}$ , it follows from $\frac{d}{dt}X(a,t)=\mathbf{u}(X(a,t),t)$ and the definition ( 2.1 ) of the self-similar transformation that $R(s)$ solves the ODE $\partial_{s}R(s)=R(s)+U(R(s),s),\quad R(s_{\mathsf{in}})=|a|T^{-\frac{1}{r}}=|a|e^{s_{\mathsf{in}}}.$ By ( 2.4d ), the assumption ( A.1 ) on $\mathbf{U}$ we get (A.3) $\partial_{s}R(s)\geq R(s)+\bar{U}(R(s))-\tfrac{1}{2}\kappa R(s)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\kappa R(s)\quad\Rightarrow\quad R(s)\geq e^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa s}R(s_{\mathsf{in}})=e^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}|a|e^{s_{\mathsf{in}}}.$ The above estimates show that $R(s)$ is increasing and diverges exponentially as $s\to\infty$ . Since by the definition of $R(s)$ we have $|X(a,t)|=R(s)e^{-s}$ , we compute $\tfrac{d}{ds}\bigl(R(s)e^{-s}\bigr)=\bigl(R(s)e^{-s}\bigr)\tfrac{U(R(s),s)}{R(s)},$ and therefore (A.4) $R(s)e^{-s}=|a|e^{I(s)},\quad\mbox{where}\quad I(s)\triangleq\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s}\tfrac{U(R(s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})}{R(s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime},$ resulting in an exact implicit formula for $e^{-s}R(s)$ .
The usefulness of ( A.4 ) is seen once we recall the decay estimate ( 2.4a ) and the assumption ( A.1 ); together with ( A.3 ), these facts imply that the exponent appearing and ( A.4 ) satisfies $|I(s)|\lesssim\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s}\min(1,R(s^{\prime})^{-c})ds^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{\infty}\min\Bigl(1,|a|^{-c}e^{-cs_{\mathsf{in}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}c\kappa(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\Bigr)ds$ where $c=\min(r,\varepsilon)>0$ .
Denoting $s_{+}=\max(s_{\mathsf{in}},(1-\frac{2}{\kappa})s_{\mathsf{in}}-\frac{2}{\kappa}\log|a|)$ , we obtain from the above estimate that $\displaystyle|I(s)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s_{+}}1ds+|a|^{-c}e^{-cs_{\mathsf{in}}}\int_{s_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2}c\kappa(s-s_{\mathsf{in}})}ds$ (A.5) $\displaystyle\lesssim s_{+}+|a|^{-c}e^{-cs_{\mathsf{in}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}c\kappa(s_{+}-s_{\mathsf{in}})}\lesssim s_{+}+e^{-\frac{1}{2}c\kappa s_{\mathsf{in}}}\leq c_{1}\max\bigl(1,-\log|a|\bigr),$ where $c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,\kappa,r,\alpha,\bar{U},T)>0$ is a constant. From ( A.4 ) and ( A.5 ) we deduce (A.6) $|X(a,t)|=R(s)e^{-s}\geq|a|e^{-|I(s)|}\geq|a|e^{-c_{1}\max(1,-\log|a|)}=|a|\min(|a|,\tfrac{1}{e})^{c_{1}},$ which concludes the proof of the lower bound in ( A.2 ). In order to prove of the uper bound in ( A.2 ), we seek an upper bound for $I(s)$ , without absolute values. Since by ( 2.4e ) we have $\partial_{\xi}\bar{U}(0)=-\frac{r-1}{2\alpha}<0$ , by the continuity of $\bar{U}$ and assumption ( A.1 ) on $\mathbf{U}$ , there exists a constant $\xi_{b}>0$ such that for all $\xi\in[0,\xi_{b}]$ we have $\bar{U}(\xi)\leq-\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{r-1}{2\alpha}\xi,\quad U(\xi)\leq\bar{U}(\xi)+|U(\xi)-\bar{U}(\xi)|\leq 0,\qquad\forall\xi\in[0,\xi_{b}].$ Then, since $R(s)$ is increasing and diverges as $s\to\infty$ (see ( A.3 )), there exists a time $s_{b}=s_{b}(|a|)\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ such that $R(s_{b})=\max(|a|e^{s_{\mathsf{in}}},\xi_{b})$ . From ( A.3 ) we obtain $R(s)\geq R(s_{b})e^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa(s-s_{b})}\geq\xi_{b}e^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa(s-s_{b})}$ for all $s\geq s_{b}$ . On the other hand, by construction we have that for $s\in[s_{\mathsf{in}},s_{b})$ we have either $s_{b}=s_{\mathsf{in}}$ or $R(s)\leq\xi_{b}$ (and hence $U(R(s),s)\leq 0$ ). Putting this information together, we deduce $I(s)=\int_{s_{\mathsf{in}}}^{s_{b}}\tfrac{U(R(s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})}{R(s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}+\int_{s_{b}}^{s}\tfrac{U(R(s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})}{R(s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}\leq\int_{s_{b}}^{s}\tfrac{|U(R(s^{\prime}),s^{\prime})|}{R(s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}.$ Using the decay estimates $|U(\xi,s)|\lesssim\xi^{1-c}$ for $c=\min(r,\varepsilon)$ (which follows from ( 2.4a ) and the assumption ( A.1 )), we get (A.7) $I(s)\lesssim\int_{s_{b}}^{s}(R(s^{\prime}))^{-c}ds^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{s_{b}}^{\infty}\bigl(\xi_{b}e^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa(s^{\prime}-s_{b})})^{-c}ds^{\prime}\lesssim 1$ where the implicit constant depends on $\varepsilon,\kappa,r,\alpha,\bar{U},T$ . We deduce $|X(a,t)|=R(s)e^{-s}=|a|e^{I(s)}\leq C|a|,$ for some $C=C(\varepsilon,\kappa,r,\alpha,\bar{U},T)\geq 1$ ,
which concludes the proof of the upper bound in ( A.2 ). In order to conclude the proof, we note that if $a=0$ , since $\mathbf{U}(0,s)=0$ , we get $X(0,t)=0$ for all $t\in[0,T)$ , and in this case the bound ( A.2 ) holds trivially. MM $\square$ Remark A.2 ( The three-dimensional case). We note that while Lemma A.1 was stated in two space dimensions, the same proof applies “as is” to the three-dimensional case; the only requirements are that the radial part of the self-similar velocity $\mathbf{U}$ can be written as $U(\xi,s)\mathbf{e}_{R}$ with $U(0,s)=0$ , $\mathbf{U}$ is at least $C^{1}$ smooth in space and time, satisfies the bound ( A.1 ), and that the profile $\bar{U}$ satisfies ( 2.4a ), ( 2.4d ), and $\partial_{\xi}\bar{U}(0)<0$ (we did not use the precise formula ( 2.4e ), only negativity of the slope near the origin). With Lemma A.1 and Remark A.2 , we turn to the proof of Lemma 2.4 . Proof of Lemma 2.4 . We adopt the notation from the proof of Lemma A.1 . Applying Lemma A.1 , respectively Remark A.2 , with $\mathbf{U}=\bar{\mathbf{U}}$ (so that $\mathbf{U}$ is smooth, radially symmetric, and trivially satisfies ( A.1 )),
we get from ( A.6 ) that (A.8) $R(s)e^{-s}\geq R_{l}(a),\qquad R_{l}(a)\triangleq|a|\min(\tfrac{1}{e},|a|)^{c_{1}},$ for all $s\geq s_{\mathsf{in}}$ and some constant $c_{1}>0$ . From ( 2.1a ), ( 2.1b ), ( 2.4a ), and ( A.8 ), we get the estimate (A.9) $\sigma(X(a,t),t)\lesssim e^{s(r-1)}\bar{\Sigma}(R(s))\lesssim e^{s(r-1)}R(s)^{-(r-1)}=(e^{-s}R(s))^{-(r-1)}\lesssim R_{l}(a)^{-(r-1)}.$ Similarly, from ( 2.1a ), ( 2.1b ), ( 2.4a ), and ( A.8 ), we have $\displaystyle|(\nabla\mathbf{u})(X(a,t),t)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim(T-t)^{-1}|(\nabla\bar{\mathbf{U}})(R(s))|\lesssim(T-t)^{-1}\langle R(s)\rangle^{-r}$ (A.10) $\displaystyle\lesssim\min((T-t)^{-1},(e^{-s}R(s))^{-r})=\min((T-t)^{-1},R_{l}(a)^{-r}).$ We now proceed to estimate $\nabla_{a}X(a,t)$ . We observe that $\nabla_{a}X(a,t)$ solves the ODE: $\partial_{t}\nabla_{a}X(a,t)=\nabla\mathbf{u}(X(t),t)\nabla_{a}X(a,t)$ , with initial data $\nabla_{a}X(a,0)={\rm Id}$ .
Introducing $t_{+}=t_{+}(a)\triangleq\max(T-R_{l}(a)^{r},0)$ we have $\min(T,R_{l}(a)^{r})\leq T-t_{+}\leq R_{l}(a)^{r}$ , and so by ( A.10 ) we find $\displaystyle|\nabla_{a}X(a,t)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}|\nabla\mathbf{u}(X(a,t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})|dt^{\prime}\right)\lesssim\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\min((T-t^{\prime})^{-1},R_{l}(a)^{-r})dt^{\prime}\Big)$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t_{+}}(T-t^{\prime})^{-1}dt^{\prime}+C\int_{t_{+}}^{T}R_{l}(a)^{-r}dt^{\prime}\Big)$ $\displaystyle=\exp(C\log T-C\log(T-t_{+})+(T-t_{+})R_{l}(a)^{-r})$ (A.11) $\displaystyle\lesssim\min(T,R_{l}(a)^{r})^{-C}\lesssim\max(1,R_{l}(a)^{-c_{2}})$ for some constant $c_{2}>0$ . In the assumption of Lemma 2.4 , we choose $k_{\mathsf{van}}\geq(c_{2}+\frac{r-1}{\alpha})(c_{1}+1)$ , where $c_{1}$ is as in ( A.8 ) and $c_{2}$ is as in ( A.11 ); with this choice, if $|f_{0}(a)|\lesssim\min(1,|a|^{k_{\mathsf{van}}})$ , since the initial data is bounded away from vacuum, by combining ( A.9 ) and ( A.11 ) we obtain $\displaystyle|\rho(X(a,t),t)\nabla_{a}X(a,t)\tfrac{f_{0}}{\rho_{0}}(a,t)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho_{0}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\max(1,R_{l}(a)^{-c_{2}-\frac{(r-1)}{\alpha}})\min(1,|a|^{k_{\mathsf{van}}})$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\max(1,|a|^{-k_{\mathsf{van}}})\min(1,|a|^{k_{\mathsf{van}}})\lesssim 1,$ uniformly for all $0\leq t<T$ and all $a\neq 0$ . If $a=0$ , then the assumption on $f_{0}$ implies $f_{0}(0)=0$ and so $\rho(X(0,t),t)\nabla_{a}X(0,t)f_{0}(0)=0$ for $0\leq t<T$ , which concludes the proof of Lemma 2.4 . MM $\square$ Appendix B Additional properties of the profile The goal of this appendix is to clarify the proof of Lemma 2.2 , based on the arguments in [ 62 ] .
For this purpose, we recall from [ 62 ] the properties of autonomous ODE for the profile $(\bar{U},\bar{\Sigma})$ . Denote (B.1) $W(x)=-\xi^{-1}\bar{U}(\xi),\quad S(x)=\alpha\bar{\Sigma}(\xi),\quad x(\xi)=\log\xi,\quad l=\tfrac{1}{\alpha}=\tfrac{2}{\gamma-1},\quad W_{e}=\tfrac{l(r-1)}{d},$ with $d=2$ . Here, the quantities $(W,S,l)$ are the same as $(w,\sigma,l)$ in [ 62 ] . The notation in ( B.1 ) is only used in this appendix, and it does not affect the rest of the paper. B.1. Repulsive properties We recall that the interior repulsive property ( 2.4b ) is proved in [ 62 , Lemma 1.6] for $\xi\in[0,\xi_{s}]$ . By continuity, there exists $\xi_{1}>\xi_{s}$ such that the condition ( 2.4b ) holds for a domain $[0,\xi_{1}]$ , thereby proving ( 2.4b ). It thus remains to prove ( 2.4a ) and ( 2.4c )–( 2.4e ).
From [ 62 , Section 2] , we have that $(W,S)$ solve the $2\times 2$ system of autonomous ODEs (B.2a) $\tfrac{dW}{dx}=-\tfrac{\Delta_{1}}{\Delta},\quad\tfrac{dS}{dx}=-\tfrac{\Delta_{2}}{\Delta},$ where (B.2b) $\displaystyle\Delta$ $\displaystyle=(1-W)^{2}-S^{2},$ (B.2c) $\displaystyle\Delta_{1}$ $\displaystyle=W(W-1)(W-r)-d(W-W_{e})S^{2}=(W-W_{1}(S))(W-W_{2}(S))(W-W_{3}(S)),$ (B.2d) $\displaystyle\Delta_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{S}{l}\Big((l+d-1)W^{2}-W(l+d+lr-r)+lr-lS^{2}\Big)=\tfrac{S(l+d-1)}{l}(W-W_{2}^{-}(S)(W-W_{2}^{+}(S)),$ where $\{W_{i}(S)\}_{i=1}^{3}$ are the roots of the cubic polynomial $\Delta_{1}$ , and $\{W_{2}^{\pm}(S)\}$ are the roots of the quadratic polynomial $\Delta_{2}$ . Here, $(W_{2}^{\pm},W_{i})$ are the same as $(w_{2}^{\pm},w_{i})$ in [ 62 ] . From [ 62 , Lemma 2.1] for the roots $W_{2}^{\pm}$ of $\Delta_{2}$ , and from [ 62 , Lemma 2.3] for the roots of $W_{i}$ for $\Delta_{1}$ , we have the following properties: (B.3a) $\displaystyle(W_{2}^{-})^{\prime}(S)<0,\quad W_{2}^{-}(S)\leq W_{2}^{+}(S),\quad\forall S>S_{2}^{(0)},$ (B.3b) $\displaystyle W_{1}^{\prime}(S)<0,\quad W_{2}^{\prime}(S)<0,\quad W_{1}(S)\leq 0<W_{e}<W_{2}(S)\leq 1<r\leq W_{3}(S),\quad\forall S>0.$ From the proof of [ 62 , Lemma 2.1] , the value $S_{2}^{(0)}\geq 0$ is chosen such that if $S_{2}^{(0)}>0$ , then for any fixed $S\in(0,S_{2}^{(0)})$ the equation $\Delta_{2}(W,S)=0$ does not have a real root $W$ . From the definition of $\Delta_{2}$ , this implies that (B.4) $\Delta_{2}(W,S)>0,\quad\forall\ S\in(0,S_{2}^{(0)}),\ W\in\mathbb{R}.$ To prove ( 2.4c ) and ( 2.4d ), we rewrite $\xi+\bar{U}(\xi)-\alpha\bar{\Sigma}(\xi),1+\xi^{-1}\bar{U}(\xi)$ as (B.5) $\xi+\bar{U}(\xi)-\alpha\bar{\Sigma}(\xi)=\xi(1-W-S),\quad\xi+\bar{U}(\xi)=\xi(1-W).$ Denote 30 30 30 We use $(Q_{S},Q_{W})$ to denote the coordinates of a point $Q$ in the system of $(S,W)$ . by $P_{2}=(P_{2,S},P_{2,W})$ and $P_{3}=(P_{3,S},P_{3,W})$ the sonic points, where $\Delta=\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{2}=0$ , and by $P_{5}=(P_{5,S},P_{5,W})$ other root of $\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{2}=0$ . In particular, we have $P_{2,S}+P_{2,W}=P_{3,S}+P_{3,W}=1$ .
From Location in [ 62 , Lemma 2.4] , $P_{2},P_{3},P_{5}$ are on the curve of the middle root $(S,W_{2}(S))$ , which along with ( B.3 ) implies (B.6) $P_{i,W}=W_{2}(P_{i,S})>0,\quad i=2,3,5.$ From Position of the middle root in [ 62 , Lemma 2.4] , we have the properties (B.7) $S+W_{2}(S)<1,\quad P_{3,S}<S<P_{2,S}.$ The following properties are proved in [ 62 , Lemma 3.2] . Lemma B.1 . Assume ( 2.2 ). Let $W(S)$ be the solution to ( B.2 ) and $W_{2}(S)$ be the middle root to $\Delta_{1}$ ( B.3 ), ( B.2 ). Suppose that $W(S)$ intersects $W_{2}(S)$ at $S^{*}$ with $P_{5,S}<S^{*}<P_{2,S}$ and $S^{*}+W(S^{*})<1$ . 31 31 31 The additional assumption $S^{*}+W(S^{*})<1$ (absent in the statement of [ 62 , Lemma 3.2] ), that the point $P_{*}\triangleq(S^{*},W(S^{*}))=(S^{*},W_{2}(S^{*}))$ lies below the sonic line, can be inferred from the phase portrait in [ 62 , Figures 1, 2] , where $P_{*}$ is the intersection between the pink and red curves. Then we have: (a) $W(S)\in C^{\infty}(0,S^{*}]$ and $\quad{\lim}_{S\to 0}W(S)=0.$ Moreover, $S\to 0$ corresponds to $x\to\infty$ and there exists $(W_{\infty},S_{\infty})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}$ such that for $x\to\infty$ , we have (B.8) $S(x)=S_{\infty}e^{-rx}(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-rx})),\quad W(x)=W_{\infty}e^{-rx}(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-rx})).\quad$ (b) $W(S)\in C^{\infty}[S^{*},P_{2,S})$ and (B.9) $W_{2}(S)<W(S)<W_{2}^{-}(S),\quad\forall S^{*}<S<P_{2,S},\quad{\lim}_{S\to(P_{2,S})^{-}}W(S)=P_{2,W}.$ Using the above properties and ( B.5 ), we are ready to prove ( 2.4c ), which is equivalent to (B.10) $1-W(S)-S>0,\quad S<P_{2,S}.$ The proof essentially justifies the phase portrait for $S<S^{*}$ in [ 62 , Figure 3] , which is omitted in [ 62 ] . Proof of ( B.10 ). From $P_{2,W}>0$ ( B.6 ) and $P_{2,S}+P_{2,W}=1$ ,
we get (B.11) $P_{2,S}<1.$ Since $W_{2}^{-}(S)$ is only defined for $S>S_{2}^{(0)}\geq 0$ , we define its extension on $(0,\infty)$ as (B.12) $W_{2,e}^{-}(S)=W_{2}(S_{2}^{(0)}),\quad S<S_{2}^{(0)},\quad W_{2,e}^{-}(S)=W_{2}^{-}(S),\quad S\geq S_{2}^{(0)}.$ Note that $S^{*}+W(S^{*})=S^{*}+W_{2}(S^{*})<1$ . If $W(\tilde{S})+\tilde{S}=1$ for some $\tilde{S}\in(S^{*},P_{2,S})$ , since the smooth solution $(S,W(S))$ can only cross the sonic line $1=W+S$ at $P_{2}$ or $P_{3}$ , where $\Delta_{2}$ vanishes, we get $\Delta_{2}(\tilde{S},W(\tilde{S}))=0$ . However, since $\tilde{S}\in(S^{*},P_{2,S})$ , from ( B.9 ), ( B.2 ), ( B.3 ), we get $W(\tilde{S})<W_{2}^{-}(\tilde{S})\leq W_{2}^{+}(\tilde{S}),\quad\Delta_{2}(S,W_{2}(\tilde{S}))>0,$ which contradicts $\Delta_{2}(\tilde{S},W(\tilde{S}))=0$ . Thus, by continuity, we get $W(S)+S<1,\quad S\in[S^{*},P_{2,S}).$ Next, we consider ( B.10 ) for $S<S^{*}$ . Since $W(S^{*})=W_{2}(S^{*})$ and $W_{2}(S)$ is a root of $\Delta_{1}(S,W)=0$ , we get $\Delta_{1}(S^{*},W(S^{*}))=0$ and $\frac{dW(S)}{dS}|_{S=S^{*}}=0$ . Moreover, using $W_{2}^{\prime}(S^{*})<0,(W_{2}^{-})^{\prime}(S^{*})<0$ ( B.3 ) and $0<W_{e}\leq W(S^{*})=W_{2}(S^{*})\leq W_{2}^{-}(S^{*})$ ( B.9 ), we obtain $W_{1}(S)\leq 0<W(S)\leq W(S^{*})<W_{2}(S),W_{2}^{-}(S),1-S,\quad S\in[S^{*}-\varepsilon,S^{*}),$ for some small $\varepsilon>0$ . Let $0\leq S_{a}<S^{*}-\varepsilon$ be the largest value, where $W(S)$ intersects the curves $W_{2}(S),W_{2,e}^{-}(S),1-S$ , or $W_{1}(S)$ . If such a point does not exist, we choose $S_{a}=0$ . Then for $S\in(S_{a},S^{*})$ , we obtain $W_{1}(S)<W(S)<W_{2}(S),W_{2,e}^{-}(S),1-S,$ which along with ( B.2 ), ( B.3 ) implies $\Delta_{2}(S,W(S))>0,\quad\Delta_{1}(S,W(S))>0,\quad\tfrac{dW}{dS}=\tfrac{\Delta_{1}}{\Delta_{2}}>0,\quad S\in(S_{a},S^{*}).$ We have $\Delta_{2}>0$ for the following reasons. If $S<S_{2}^{(0)}$ , we obtain $\Delta_{2}>0$ from ( B.4 ); if $S\geq S_{2}^{(0)}$ , from ( B.12 ), we obtain $W(S)<W_{2,e}^{-}(S)=W_{2}^{-}(S)$ , which along with ( B.2 ), ( B.3 ) implies $\Delta_{2}>0$ . The monotonicity of $W(S)$ implies $W(S)<W(S^{*}),S\in(S_{a},S^{*})$ . For $S\in(S_{a},S^{*})$ , we prove $S+W(S)<W(S^{*})+S^{*}<1.$ If $S_{a}=0$ , we prove ( B.10 ). If $S_{a}>0$ , since $W_{2}(S),W_{2,e}^{-}(S),1-S$ are decreasing on $(S_{a},S^{*})$ , ( B.3 ) and $W(S)$ is increasing on $(S_{a},S^{*})$ , $W(S)$ cannot intersect these three curves at $S_{a}$ . Therefore, $W(S)$ must intersect $W_{1}(S)$ at $S_{a}$ : $W(S_{a})=W_{1}(S_{a})$ . Since $W_{1}^{\prime}(S)<0$ ( B.3 ), $W_{1}(S)$ is a root of $\Delta_{1}(S,W)$ , and $\Delta_{2}(S_{a},W(S_{a}))\neq 0$ (the double roots satisfies $P_{i,W}>0\geq W(S_{a})$ ( B.6 )) $\tfrac{dW}{dS}|_{S=S_{a}}=0,\quad W(S_{a})=W_{1}(S_{a}),$ and $\Delta(W,S)>0$ for $W\leq 0,S\leq S^{*}<P_{2,S}<1$ ( B.11 ), we get $W(S)-W_{1}(S)<0$ for $S\in(S_{a}-\varepsilon_{2},S_{a})$ with some small $\varepsilon_{2}$ .
If $W(S_{I})=W_{1}(S_{I})\leq 0$ for some $S_{I}\in(0,S_{a})$ , since the roots $P_{i}$ of $\Delta_{1}=0,\Delta_{2}=0$ satisfies $P_{i,W}>0$ ( B.6 ), we obtain $\Delta_{2}(S_{I},W_{1}(S_{I}))\neq 0$ . Thus, using $\tfrac{d(W-W_{1})}{dS}\Big|_{S=S_{I}}=\tfrac{\Delta_{1}(S_{I},W_{1}(S_{I}))}{\Delta_{2}(S_{I},W_{1}(S_{I}))}-W_{1}^{\prime}(S_{I})=0-W_{1}^{\prime}(S_{I})>0,$ we get that $W_{1}(S)$ is a upper barrier of $W(S)$ for $S\in(0,S_{a})$ . From ( B.3 )
and ( B.7 ), for $S\leq S_{a}<S^{*}<P_{2,S}<1$ , we get $W(S)\leq W_{1}(S)\leq 0,\ W(S)+S\leq S<1.$ We complete the proof of ( B.10 ). MM $\square$ The following property of $W(S)$ for $S>P_{2,S}$ is proved in item (2) Original variables and item (3) Reaching $P_{2}$ in [ 62 , Lemma 3.1] . Lemma B.2 . Assume ( 2.2 ). We have $W(S)\in C^{\infty}(P_{2,S},\infty)$ and $W_{2}^{-}(S)<W(S)<W_{2}(S),\quad{\lim}_{S\to(P_{2,S})^{+}}W(S)=P_{2,W},\quad{\lim}_{S\to\infty}W(S)=W_{e}.$ For $S_{0}>P_{2,S}$ large enough, the curve $(S,W(S))$ with $S>S_{0}$ corresponds to the
spherically symmetric profile $(\bar{\Sigma}(|y|),\bar{\mathbf{U}}(y))$ (see Theorem 2.1 ) defined on $|y|\in[0,y_{0}]$ for some $y_{0}>0$ . Proof of ( 2.4d ) and ( 2.4e ) Below, we prove ( 2.4d ), ( 2.4e ). Since $W_{e},W_{2}(P_{2,S})=P_{2,W}=1-P_{2,S}<1,W_{2}(S)\leq 1$ ( B.3 ),
using Lemma B.2 , we obtain $W(S)<c<1,\qquad\forall S\geq P_{2,S}$ for some $c>0$ . For $0<S<P_{2,S}$ , using ( B.10 ) and continuity, we obtain $W(S)<c_{2}<1,\qquad\forall S<P_{2,S}.$ By combining the above two inequalities, we have thus proven that $W(S)\leq c_{3}<1$ for any $S\geq 0$ . Using ( B.5 ), the inequality ( 2.4d ) now follows. From ( B.1 ), we have $W_{e}=\frac{r-1}{2\alpha}$ for $d=2$ . Since $S(x)\to\infty$ corresponds to $\xi\to 0$ , using Lemma B.2 and $W(x)=-\frac{\bar{U}}{\xi}$ ( B.1 ), we obtain ${\lim}_{\xi\to 0}\xi^{-1}\bar{U}(\xi)=-\lim_{S\to\infty}W(S)=-W_{e}=-\tfrac{r-1}{2\alpha}.$ The bound $-\frac{r-1}{2\alpha}>-\frac{r}{2}$ follows from ( 2.2 ). We establish ( 2.4e ). B.2. Decay of the profile for large $\xi$ In this section, we prove ( 2.4a ). For $x>x_{l}$ with $x_{l}$ sufficiently large, using ( B.8 ) in Lemma B.1 , we obtain $|W|\ll 1,|S|\ll 1,\Delta>\frac{1}{2}$ . We focus on this region.
For $x>x_{l}$ , we use induction on $k\geq 0$ to prove (B.13) $|\partial_{x}^{k}W|\lesssim_{k}\min(1,e^{-rx}),\quad|\partial_{x}^{k}S|\lesssim_{k}\min(1,e^{-rx}).$ The base case $k=0$ follows from ( B.8 ). Suppose that ( B.13 ) holds for $k\leq n-1,n\geq 1$ . To estimate $\partial_{x}^{n-1}W$ , we take $\partial_{x}^{n-1}$ on both sides of the ODEs ( B.2 ) for $W,S$ .
Since $\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2},\Delta$ ( B.2 ) are polynomials of $W,S$ , using Leibniz’s rule, the inductive hypothesis, and $(\min(1,e^{-rx}))^{l}\leq\min(1,e^{-rx}),l\geq 1$ , we yield $|\partial_{x}^{i}\Delta_{1}|,|\partial_{x}^{i}\Delta_{2}|,|\partial_{x}^{j}\Delta|\lesssim_{n}\min(1,e^{-rx}),\quad 0\leq i\leq n-1,1\leq j\leq n-1.$ We need $j\geq 1$ so that the constant $1$ in $\Delta$ vanishes in $\partial_{x}^{j}\Delta$ ( B.2 ).
Since $\Delta\geq\frac{1}{2}$ , using Leibniz’s rule again, we obtain $|\partial_{x}^{n}W|=\bigl|\partial_{x}^{n-1}\bigl(\tfrac{\Delta_{1}}{\Delta}\bigr)\bigr|\lesssim_{n}\min(1,e^{-rx}),\quad|\partial_{x}^{n}S|=\bigl|\partial_{x}^{n-1}\bigl(\tfrac{\Delta_{2}}{\Delta}\bigr)\bigr|\lesssim_{n}\min(1,e^{-rx}).$ Note that in the full expansion of $\partial_{x}^{n-1}(\Delta_{i}/\Delta),i=1,2$ , the numerator only involves $\partial_{x}^{j}\Delta_{1},j\geq 1$ . We prove the estimates for the case of $k=n$ and establish ( B.13 ) by induction. Using the relations $\partial_{x}W(x)=\xi\partial_{\xi}W(x(\xi))$ , $W(x)=-\xi^{-1}\bar{U}(\xi)$ , and $x=\log\xi$ (see ( B.1 )), for $\xi>e^{x_{l}}$ , we obtain $\bigl|(\xi\partial_{\xi})^{i}\bigl(\xi^{-1}\bar{U}(\xi)\bigr)\bigr|\lesssim_{i}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r},\quad i\geq 0.$ Applying the above estimate and the Leibniz rule to $\bar{U}=(\bar{U}/\xi)\cdot\xi$ , we obtain $|(\xi\partial_{\xi})^{i}\bar{U}|\lesssim_{i}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r+1}$ for $\xi>e^{x_{l}}$ .
Using induction on $i$ , we further obtain $|\partial_{\xi}^{i}\bar{U}|\lesssim_{i}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r+1-i}$ for $\xi>e^{x_{l}}$ . Since $\bar{U}\in C^{\infty}$ ,
for $\xi\leq e^{x_{l}}$ , the bound $|\partial_{\xi}^{i}\bar{U}|\lesssim_{i}\langle\xi\rangle^{-r+1-i}$ is trivial. The decay estimate of $\bar{\Sigma}$ is similar, thereby proving ( 2.4a ). Appendix C Functional inequalities The goal of this appendix is to gather a few functional analytic bounds that are used throughout the paper. First, we record a Leibniz rule for radially symmetric vectors/scalars. Lemma C.1 (​ [ 5 ] ) . Let $f,g$ be radially symmetric scalar functions over $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ and let $\mathbf{F}=F\mathbf{e}_{R}=(F_{1},..,F_{d})$ and $\mathbf{G}=G\mathbf{e}_{R}=(G_{1},..,G_{d})$ be radially symmetric vector fields over $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ . For integers $m\geq 1$ we have $\displaystyle|\Delta^{m}(\mathbf{F}\cdot\nabla G_{i})-\mathbf{F}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m}G_{i}-2m\partial_{\xi}F\;\Delta^{m}G_{i}|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2m}|\nabla^{2m+1-j}\mathbf{F}|\cdot|\nabla^{j}G_{i}|,$ $\displaystyle|\Delta^{m}(f\nabla g)-f\nabla\Delta^{m}g-2m\nabla f\Delta^{m}g|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2m}|\nabla^{2m+1-j}f|\cdot|\nabla^{j}g|,$ $\displaystyle|\Delta^{m}(\mathbf{F}\cdot\nabla g)-\mathbf{F}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{m}g-2m\partial_{\xi}F\;\Delta^{m}g|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2m}|\nabla^{2m+1-j}\mathbf{F}|\cdot|\nabla^{j}g|$ $\displaystyle|\Delta^{m}(f\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{G})-f\operatorname{div}(\Delta^{m}\mathbf{G})-2m\nabla f\cdot\Delta^{m}\mathbf{G}|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim_{m}\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2m}|\nabla^{2m+1-j}f|\cdot|\nabla^{j}\mathbf{G}|,$ whenever $f,g,\{F_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d},\{G_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}$ are sufficiently smooth. Lemma C.1 was established for $d=3$ in the proof of [ 5 , Lemma A.4] . The proof given in [ 5 ] also works for any dimension $d\geq 2$ , so we do not repeat these arguments here. Next, we focus on Gagliardo-Nirenberg-type interpolation bounds with weights . In all of the following lemmas, we do not assume that the functions are radially symmetric. Lemma C.2 . Let $\delta_{1}\in(0,1]$ and $\delta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}$ . For integers $n\geq 0$ and sufficiently smooth functions $f$ on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ , we denote (C.1) $\beta_{n}\triangleq 2n\delta_{1}+\delta_{2},\qquad I_{n}\triangleq\int|\nabla^{n}f(y)|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}}dy,\qquad J_{n}\triangleq I_{n}+I_{0},$ where as usual we let $\langle y\rangle=(1+|y|^{2})^{1/2}$ .
Then, for $n<m$ and for $\varepsilon>0$ , there exists a constant $C_{\varepsilon,n,m}=C(\varepsilon,n,m,\delta_{1},\delta_{2},d)>0$ such that (C.2a) $I_{n}\leq\varepsilon I_{m}+C_{\varepsilon,n,m}I_{0}.$ Moreover, for $p<r<q$ we have the following interpolation inequality on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ : (C.2b) $J_{r}\leq C_{p,q,r}J_{p}^{\alpha}J_{q}^{1-\alpha},\qquad\mbox{where}\qquad\alpha=\tfrac{q-r}{q-p},$ for some constant $C_{p,q,r}=C(p,q,r,\delta_{1},\delta_{2},d)>0$ . Proof. Throughout the proof, all implicit constants depend on the index $n$ of $I_{n}$ or $J_{n}$ , and on the parameters $\delta_{1},\delta_{2}$ .
Using integration by parts, we have $\displaystyle I_{n}$ $\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\int\partial_{i}\nabla^{n-1}f\cdot\partial_{i}\nabla^{n-1}f\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}}$ $\displaystyle=-\sum_{i}\int\bigl(\Delta\nabla^{n-1}f\cdot\nabla^{n-1}f\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}}+\beta_{n}\partial_{i}\nabla^{n-1}f\cdot\nabla^{n-1}fy_{i}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}-2}\bigr)$ Using that $\delta_{1}\leq 1$ we have $\beta_{n}-1\leq\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{n-1}+\beta_{n})$ , while by definition we have $\beta_{n}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\beta_{n-1}+\beta_{n+1})$ ; thus, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we obtain (C.3) $I_{n}\leq(I_{n+1}I_{n-1})^{1/2}+\beta_{n}(I_{n}I_{n-1})^{1/2},\quad n\geq 1.$ To prove ( C.2a ), it is sufficient to show that for any $n\geq 0$ and $\varepsilon>0$ , there exists $C_{\varepsilon,n}>0$ such that (C.4) $I_{n}\leq\varepsilon I_{n+1}+C_{\varepsilon,n}I_{0}.$ We prove ( C.4 ) by induction on $n$ . The base case $n=0$ is trivial, with $C_{\varepsilon,0}=1$ .
For the induction step, let $n\geq 1$ . We use ( C.3 ), the inductive hypothesis for $n-1$ , and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, to conclude $I_{n}\leq\varepsilon I_{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{2}I_{n}+C_{\varepsilon,n}I_{n-1}\leq\varepsilon I_{n+1}+\tfrac{3}{4}I_{n}+C_{\varepsilon,n}I_{0}.$ The above estimate concludes the proof of the induction step for ( C.4 ); thus, we have proven ( C.2a ). Combining ( C.3 ) and ( C.4 ) we obtain $I_{n}\leq(I_{n+1}I_{n-1})^{1/2}+\tfrac{1}{2}I_{n}+C_{n}I_{n-1}\leq(I_{n+1}I_{n-1})^{1/2}+\tfrac{3}{4}I_{n}+C_{n}I_{0}.$ It follows that for all $n\geq 1$ , $J_{n}=I_{n}+I_{0}\leq 4(I_{n+1}I_{n-1})^{1/2}+C_{n}I_{0}\leq C_{n}J_{n+1}^{1/2}J_{n-1}^{1/2}.$ Iterating the above estimate, we deduce ( C.2b ). MM $\square$ Lemma C.3 . Let $\delta_{1}\in(0,1],\delta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}$ , and define $\beta_{n}=2n\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}$ . Let $\tilde{\varphi}_{n}$ be a weight satisfying the pointwise properties $\tilde{\varphi}_{n}(y)\asymp_{n}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}}$ and $|\nabla\tilde{\varphi}_{n}(y)|\lesssim_{n}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}-1}$ . Then, for any $\varepsilon>0$ and $n\geq 0$ , there exists a constant $C_{\varepsilon,n}=C(\varepsilon,n,\delta_{1},\delta_{2},d)>0$ such that 32 32 32 Throughout the paper we denote by $|\nabla^{k}f|$ the Euclidean norm of the $k$ -tensor $\nabla^{k}f$ , namely, $|\nabla^{k}f|=(\sum_{|\alpha|=k}|\partial^{\alpha}f|^{2})^{1/2}$ . $\int|\nabla^{2n}f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\int|\Delta^{n}f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}+C_{\varepsilon,n}\int|f|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{0}},$ for any function $f$ on $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ which is sufficiently smooth and has suitable decay at infinity. Proof. We use the standard summation convention on repeated indices. Using integration by parts, we get $\displaystyle B_{n}$ $\displaystyle\triangleq\int|\Delta^{n}f|^{2}\varphi_{2n}=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}}\int\partial_{i_{1}}^{2}\partial_{i_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{i_{n}}^{2}f\;\partial_{j_{1}}^{2}\partial_{j_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\;\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}$ $\displaystyle=-\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}}\int\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{i_{n}}^{2}f\;\bigl(\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{j_{1}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\;\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}+\partial_{j_{1}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\;\partial_{i_{1}}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}}\int\partial_{i_{1}j_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{i_{n}}^{2}f\;\partial_{i_{1}j_{1}}\partial_{j_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\;\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}$ $\displaystyle+\int\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{i_{n}}^{2}f\bigl(\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{j_{1}}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\partial_{j_{1}}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}-\partial_{j_{1}}^{2}\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}^{2}f\;\partial_{i_{1}}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}\bigr)\triangleq I+II.$ The term $II$ in the above identity involves a derivative of the weight and we do not further perform integration by parts for it. For the first term, we iteratively integrate by parts with respect to $\partial_{i_{l}}^{2}$ and $\partial_{j_{l}}^{2}$ , with $l\in\{2,\ldots,n\}$ , to obtain $B_{n}\geq\int|\nabla^{2n}f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}-C_{n}\int|\nabla^{2n-1}f|\;|\nabla^{2n}f|\;|\nabla\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}|.$ By assumption we have $\delta_{1}\leq 1$ , so that $|\nabla\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}|\lesssim_{n}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{2n}-1}\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{2n-1}/2}\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{2n}/2}\lesssim|\tilde{\varphi}_{2n-1}|^{1/2}|\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}|^{1/2}.$ Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for any $\varepsilon>0$ we obtain $B_{n}\geq(1-\varepsilon)\int|\nabla^{2n}f|^{2}\varphi_{2n}-C_{n,\varepsilon}\int|\nabla^{2n-1}f|^{2}\varphi_{2n-1}.$ In analogy to the proof of ( C.2a ) in Lemma C.2 (we need to replace the weight $\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{n}}$ with $\tilde{\varphi}_{n}$ , which is permissible in light of the assumed properties of $\tilde{\varphi}_{n}$ ), we may further show that for any $\varepsilon^{\prime}>0$ , $\int|\nabla^{2n-1}f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n-1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\int|\nabla^{2n}f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{2n}+C_{\varepsilon^{\prime},n}\int|f|^{2}\tilde{\varphi}_{0}.$ Since $\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}>0$ are arbitrary and $\tilde{\varphi}_{0}\asymp\langle y\rangle^{\beta_{0}}$ , rewriting the above two inequalities completes the proof. MM $\square$ Lastly, we record the following product estimates for the functional spaces ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}$ defined in ( 3.14 ). It is convenient to state estimates for a general dimension $d$ , not just for $d=2$ . For this purpose, we recall from ( 3.15 ) that $\varphi_{g}=\langle y\rangle^{-\kappa_{1}-d}$ , with $\kappa_{1}=1/4\in(0,1)$ . Moreover, we recall from Lemma 3.1 that the weights $\varphi_{m}$ satisfy $\varphi_{m}(y)\asymp_{m}\langle y\rangle^{m}$ , and $|\nabla\varphi_{m}(y)|\lesssim_{m}\varphi_{m-1}(y)$ . Lemma C.4 . Let $m\geq d/2$ .
For $f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}$ which lies in ${\mathcal{X}}^{m}$ and $0\leq i\leq 2m-d$ , we have (C.5) $|\nabla^{i}f(y)|\lesssim_{m}\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-i+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}},$ pointwise for $y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$ .
Moreover, for any $i\leq 2m$ , $j\leq 2n$ , with $i\leq 2m-d$ or $j\leq 2n-d$ , and for any $\beta\geq\kappa_{1}/2$ , we have (C.6) $\|\nabla^{i}f\;\nabla^{j}g\;\langle y\rangle^{i+j-\beta}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m,n,\beta}\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\|g\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{n}}.$ Proof. Fix $1\leq i\leq 2m-d$ .
By a standard density argument we can assume that $\nabla^{i}f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$ .
Consider the cone with vertex at $y$ extending towards infinity: $\Omega(y)\triangleq\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon z_{j}\mathrm{sgn}(y_{j})\geq|y_{j}|,\forall 1\leq j\leq d\}$ . In particular, for any $z\in\Omega(y)$ have $|z|\geq|y|$ . By integrating on rays extending to infinity, we have $|\nabla^{i}f(y)|\lesssim\int_{\Omega(y)}|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}..\partial_{d}\nabla^{i}f(z)|dz.$ Upon letting $\delta_{2}=-(\kappa_{1}+d)$ and $\delta_{1}=1$ (choices with are consistent with the weights in ( 3.14 )), from Cauchy-Schwartz, Lemma C.2 with $\beta_{n}=2n+\delta_{2}$ (applicable since $i+d\leq 2m$ ), and Lemma C.3 with $\tilde{\varphi}_{n}=\varphi_{n}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\asymp\langle y\rangle^{n}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}$ , we deduce $\displaystyle|\nabla^{i}f(y)|$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle z\rangle^{i+d+\frac{\delta_{2}}{2}}\nabla^{i+d}f(z)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y))}\Big(\int_{|z|\geq|y|}\langle z\rangle^{-2i-2d-\delta_{2}}dz\Big)^{1/2}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle z\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{i+d}}\nabla^{i+d}f(z)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\int_{|y|}^{\infty}\langle R\rangle^{-2i-2d+(\kappa_{1}+d)}R^{d-1}dR\Big)^{1/2}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\langle z\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{2m}}\nabla^{2m}f(z)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\langle z\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}f(z)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\Big(\int_{|y|}^{\infty}\langle R\rangle^{-2i+\kappa_{1}-1}d\langle R\rangle\Big)^{1/2}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{-i+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}.$ For the last inequality we have crucially used that $2i-\kappa_{1}>0$ , which holds since $i\geq 1$ . For $i=0$ , the aforementioned inequality (namely, $2i-\kappa_{1}>0$ ) does not hold, and so the integral appearing in the above estimates does not converge. Instead, using the fundamental theorem of calculus and the decay estimate for $|\nabla f|$ obtained above, we have $|f(y)|\leq|f(0)|+\int_{0}^{|y|}|\nabla f|\Big(\frac{ty}{|y|}\Big)dt\lesssim|f(0)|+\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\int_{0}^{|y|}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}dt\lesssim|f(0)|+\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}.$ To conclude the proof of ( C.5 ) for $i=0$ , we note that $|f(0)|\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}$ . To see this, let $\chi$ be a smooth bump function $0\leq\chi\leq 1$ with $\chi\equiv 1$ for $|y|\leq 1/2$ and $\chi\equiv 0$ for $|y|\geq 1$ . Then, we have $|f(0)|\leq\|\chi f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\chi f\|_{H^{2m}}\lesssim\|\chi f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}$ , where the last inequality holds by Lemma C.2 and Lemma C.3 . In order to prove ( C.6 ), assume without loss of generality that $i\leq 2m-d$ . Using ( C.5 ), and recalling that $\varphi_{g}=\langle y\rangle^{-\kappa_{1}-d}$ , we have $\|\nabla^{i}f\nabla^{j}g\langle y\rangle^{i+j-\beta}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\|\langle y\rangle^{i-\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\nabla^{i}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{j}g\langle y\rangle^{j-\beta+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\varphi_{g}^{1/2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\|f\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}}\|\nabla^{j}g\langle y\rangle^{j-\beta-\frac{d}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.$ Next, Lemma C.2 with $\delta_{1}=1$ and $\delta_{2}=-(\kappa_{1}+d)$ , to obtain $\|\nabla^{j}g\langle y\rangle^{j-\beta-\frac{d}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{j}g\langle y\rangle^{j+\frac{\delta_{2}}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\|g\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{m}},$ where we have used that $-\beta-d/2\leq\delta_{2}/2$ , which holds since $\beta\geq\kappa_{1}/2$ .       support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below: Click the "Report Issue" ( ) button, located in the page header. Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report. Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all. Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion , and welcome developer contributions . We gratefully acknowledge support from our major funders , member institutions , ,
